\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{C}}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{t},\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})+({\mathbf{C}}[h^{t}]-{\mathbf{C}}^{t})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\lambda}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{C}}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{t},\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})+\|{\mathbf{C}}[h^{t}]-{\mathbf{C}}^{t}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{\lambda}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{C}}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{t},\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})+a\sqrt{d}\rho^{\prime}.

Further,

\displaystyle\widehat{f}^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t})

\displaystyle=\min_{{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathcal{C}}}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t}}^{\top}{\mathbf{C}}+\min_{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\in\Delta_{d}}\psi({\boldsymbol{\xi}})-{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}+{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\phi}}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})

\displaystyle\geq{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t}}^{\top}{\mathbf{C}}^{t}-a(\rho+\sqrt{d}\rho^{\prime})+\psi({\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{t})-{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{t}+{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\phi}}({\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{t})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{C}}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{t},\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t})-a(\rho+\sqrt{d}\rho^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{C}}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{t},\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})+(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\lambda})^{\top}({\mathbf{C}}^{t}-{\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{t})+({\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}-{\boldsymbol{\mu}})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\phi}}({\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{t})-a(\rho+\sqrt{d}\rho^{\prime})

\displaystyle\geq\widehat{f}^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})-a\sqrt{d}\rho^{\prime}+(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\lambda})^{\top}({\mathbf{C}}^{t}-{\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{t})+({\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}-{\boldsymbol{\mu}})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\phi}}({\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{t})-a(\rho+\sqrt{d}\rho^{\prime}),

as desired. If
[\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}]\notin\mathcal{R}^{0}
, the result follows trivially.

Equipped with lemmas
39
,
42
and
40
, we are now ready to prove that Algorithm
7
approximately maximizes
f^{\textup{{con}}}
. The monograph by
Bubeck 2015
presents a proof to derive the sub-optimality of the regular ellipsoid algorithm, where perfect (sub/ super) gradient access is assumed. In our setting, we only have access to approximate super-gradients. We show how to adapt the proof of
Bubeck 2015
to our setting, in the proof for Theorem
44
.

Lemma 43
.

Let
\tau=a(\rho+2\sqrt{d}\rho^{\prime})
. For any
t\in\{0,1,\ldots,T-1\}
, such that,
[\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}]\in\mathcal{R}^{0}

{\mathcal{E}}^{t}\setminus{\mathcal{E}}^{t+1}\subset\{[\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}]\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d+K}:\widehat{f}^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})\leq\widehat{f}^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t})+\tau\}

{proof}

Pick
t\in\{0,1,\ldots,T-1\}
, such that
[\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}]\in\mathcal{R}^{0}
. We know by lemma
42
that
{\mathbf{g}}^{t}:=[{\mathbf{C}}^{t}-{\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\phi}}({\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{t})]
is a
\tau
super-gradient to
\widehat{f}^{\textup{{con}}}
at
[\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}]
. Thus,
\forall\hskip 1.49994pt\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},\hskip 3.00003pt\forall\hskip 1.49994pt{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{K}
, we have that

\widehat{f}^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})\leq\widehat{f}^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t})+(g^{t})^{\top}([\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}]-[\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}])+\tau

(31)

Since
[\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}]\in\mathcal{R}^{0}
, the
else
condition (line 11) of Algorithm
7
gets invoked and we get that
{\mathcal{E}}^{t+1}=\textup{{JLE}}({\mathcal{E}}^{t},g^{t})
and thus by Lemma
34
and Equation (
31
) we have the following:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle\supseteq{\mathcal{E}}^{t}\cap\{[\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}]\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d+K}:({\mathbf{g}}^{t})^{\top}([\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}]-[\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}])\geq 0\}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}^{t}\setminus{\mathcal{E}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle\subseteq\{[\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}]\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d+K}:({\mathbf{g}}^{t})^{\top}([\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}]-[\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}])<0\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\{[\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}]\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d+K}:\widehat{f}^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})\leq\widehat{f}^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t})+\tau\}

where the second line follows from the argument that for any sets
A,B,C
, if
A\supset B\cap C
then
B\setminus A\subseteq C^{c}
, and the last line follows from Equation (
31
).

Theorem 44
.

Let the assumptions stated in Theorem
18
hold. Then,

\max_{0\leq t\leq T-1}\widehat{f}^{\textup{{con}}}({\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}})\geq\max_{\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}\widehat{f}^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})-\big(4a\sqrt{d+K}\big)\cdot\exp\left(\frac{-T}{2(d+K)^{2}}\right)-\tau

where
\tau=a(\rho+2\sqrt{d}\rho^{\prime})

{proof}

Due to lemma
38
, we know that
\exists\hskip 3.00003pt[\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{*}]\in\mathcal{R}^{0}
, where
[\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{*}]
is a maximizer of
f^{\textup{{con}}}
over
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{K}_{+}
. Set
\epsilon=2\exp\left(\frac{-T}{2(d+K)^{2}}\right)
which implies
T>2(d+K)^{2}log(\frac{2}{\epsilon})
. Let
{\mathcal{R}}^{0}_{\epsilon}\subseteq{\mathcal{R}}^{0}\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{K}
be

\mathcal{R}^{0}_{\epsilon}:=\{[\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}]\in\mathcal{R}^{0}:(1-\epsilon)[\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{*}]+\epsilon[\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}]\}.

By Lemma
39
, there exists an iteration
t^{*}\in\{0,1,\ldots,T-1\}
, such that,

\mathcal{R}^{0}_{\epsilon}\subseteq{\mathcal{E}}^{t^{*}},\hskip 3.00003pt

\mathcal{R}^{0}_{\epsilon}\not\subseteq{\mathcal{E}}^{t^{*}+1}
and
[\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t^{*}},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t^{*}}]\in\mathcal{R}^{0}
. Pick any element
[\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\epsilon},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\epsilon}]\in\mathcal{R}^{0}_{\epsilon}\setminus{{\mathcal{E}}}^{t^{*}+1}\subseteq{\mathcal{E}}^{t^{*}}\setminus{{\mathcal{E}}}^{t^{*}+1}
. Because of the definition of
\mathcal{R}^{0}_{\epsilon},\hskip 3.00003pt\exists\hskip 1.49994pt[\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{{}^{\prime}},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}{{}^{\prime}}]\in\mathcal{R}^{0}
, such that,
[\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\epsilon},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\epsilon}]=(1-\epsilon)[\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{*}]+\epsilon[\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{{}^{\prime}},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}{{}^{\prime}}]
. Due to Lemma
43
, we have that,

\displaystyle\widehat{f}^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t^{*}},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t^{*}})

\displaystyle\geq\widehat{f}^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\epsilon},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\epsilon})-\tau

\displaystyle=f^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\epsilon},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\epsilon})-\tau

\displaystyle=f^{\textup{{con}}}((1-\epsilon)\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*}+\epsilon\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{{}^{\prime}},(1-\epsilon){\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{*}+\epsilon{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{{}^{\prime}})-\tau

\displaystyle\geq(1-\epsilon)f^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{*})+\epsilon f^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{{}^{\prime}},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{{}^{\prime}})-\tau

\displaystyle\geq(1-\epsilon)f^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{*})+\epsilon(f^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{*})-2a\sqrt{d+K})-\tau

\displaystyle=f^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{*})-\epsilon(2a\sqrt{d+K})-\tau

\displaystyle=f^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{*})-4a\sqrt{d+K}\exp\left(\frac{-T}{2(d+K)^{2}}\right)-\tau

the second inequality in the above argument is due to the concavity of
f^{\textup{{con}}}
and the third inequality is due to the
\sqrt{d+K}
Lipschitzness of
f^{\textup{{con}}}
in
{\mathcal{R}}^{0}
(Lemma
40
) and the
\ell_{2}
-norm diameter of the set
{\mathcal{R}}^{0}
being bounded above by
2a
. The theorem follows from the equality of
f^{\textup{{con}}}
and
\widehat{f}^{\textup{{con}}}
within
{\mathcal{R}}^{0}
.

A.10.2
Converting guarantee on
\widehat{f}^{\textup{{con}}}
to primal optimality-feasibility guarantees

Now, we can bound the primal sub-optimality using a standard technique from optimization theory
Lee et al. 2015
. Throughout, we will appeal to the high-probability inequalities established in
41
.

Lemma 45
.

Denote
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}=\textup{conv}(\{{\mathbf{C}}[h^{0}],{\mathbf{C}}[h^{1}],\ldots,{\mathbf{C}}[h^{T-1}]\})
. We then have:

\displaystyle\min_{{\mathbf{C}}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{C}},{\boldsymbol{\phi}}({\mathbf{C}})\leq 0}\psi({\mathbf{C}})

\displaystyle\leq\min_{{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathcal{C}},{\boldsymbol{\phi}}({\mathbf{C}})\leq 0}\psi({\mathbf{C}})+\big(4a\sqrt{d+K}\big)\cdot\exp\left(\frac{-T}{2(d+K)^{2}}\right)+2\tau

where
\tau=a(\rho+2\sqrt{d}\rho^{\prime})
.

{proof}

Consider an alternative version of
f^{\textup{{con}}}
defined as

\widetilde{f}^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})=\min_{{\mathbf{C}}\in\widetilde{\mathbf{C}},{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\in[0,1]^{d}}\mathcal{L}^{\textup{{con}}}({\mathbf{C}},\xi,\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}).

And let
\widehat{\widetilde{f}}^{\textup{{con}}}
be equal to
\widetilde{f}^{\textup{{con}}}
if its argument
\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
is inside the
\ell_{2}
-norm ball of radius
a
and
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\geq 0
, and negative infinity otherwise.

Clearly we have that
\widehat{\widetilde{f}}^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})\geq\widehat{f}^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})
. We can also show
\widehat{\widetilde{f}}^{\textup{{con}}}
and
\widehat{f}^{\textup{{con}}}
are close at the iterates
[\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}]
. If
[\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}]\notin\mathcal{R}^{0}
, then both sides are trivially equal to negative infinity. Suppose
[\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}]\in\mathcal{R}^{0}
, we then have:

\displaystyle\widehat{\widetilde{f}}^{\textup{{con}}}({\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}})={\widetilde{f}}^{\textup{{con}}}({\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{C}}[h^{t}],{\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t}},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t})

\displaystyle=\psi({\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{t})-{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{t}+{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t}}^{\top}{\mathbf{C}}[h^{t}]+{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\phi}}({\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{t})

\displaystyle=\min_{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\in\Delta_{d}}\left(\psi({\boldsymbol{\xi}})-{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}+{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\phi}}({\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{t})\right)+{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t}}^{\top}{\mathbf{C}}[h^{t}]

\displaystyle\leq\min_{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\in\Delta_{d}}\left(\psi({\boldsymbol{\xi}})-{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}+{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\phi}}({\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{t})\right)+\min_{{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathcal{C}}}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t}}^{\top}{\mathbf{C}}+a\rho

\displaystyle=\min_{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\in\Delta_{d},{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathcal{C}}}\left(\psi({\boldsymbol{\xi}})+{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t}}^{\top}({\mathbf{C}}-{\boldsymbol{\xi}})+{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\phi}}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})\right)+a\rho

\displaystyle=f^{\textup{{con}}}({\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t}},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t})+a\rho=\widehat{f}^{\textup{{con}}}({\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t}},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t})+a\rho.

(32)

From Lemma
38
and the min-max theorem, we have the following:

\displaystyle\max_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{K}_{+}}\widehat{\widetilde{f}}^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})

\displaystyle=\max_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{K}_{+}}\widetilde{f}^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})

\displaystyle=\max_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{K}_{+}}\min_{{\mathbf{C}}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{C}},{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\in\Delta_{d}}\mathcal{L}^{\textup{{con}}}({\mathbf{C}},\xi,\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})

\displaystyle=\min_{{\mathbf{C}}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{C}},{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\in\Delta_{d}}\max_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{K}_{+}}\mathcal{L}^{\textup{{con}}}({\mathbf{C}},\xi,\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})

\displaystyle=\min_{{\mathbf{C}}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{C}},{\boldsymbol{\phi}}({\mathbf{C}})\leq 0}\psi({\mathbf{C}}).

(33)

Recall that Algorithm
7
is designed to find the minimum of
\psi
over
{\mathcal{C}}
(subject to constraints
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}
). However, the exact same sequence of iterates would also apply for minimizing ove
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}
, and hence the sequence of iterates
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}}
also approximately maximise
\widetilde{f}^{\textup{{con}}}
. Then by Theorem
44
and Equation (
32
) we have,

\displaystyle\max_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{K}_{+}}\widehat{\widetilde{f}}^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})

\displaystyle\leq\max_{0\leq t\leq T}\widehat{\widetilde{f}}^{\textup{{con}}}({\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}})+\big(4a\sqrt{d+K}\big)\cdot\exp\left(\frac{-T}{2(d+K)^{2}}\right)+\tau

\displaystyle\leq\max_{0\leq t\leq T}\widehat{f}^{\textup{{con}}}({\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{t},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{t}})+a\rho+\big(4a\sqrt{d+K}\big)\cdot\exp\left(\frac{-T}{2(d+K)^{2}}\right)+\tau

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{K}_{+}}\widehat{f}^{\textup{{con}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda},{\boldsymbol{\mu}})+\big(4a\sqrt{d+K}\big)\cdot\exp\left(\frac{-T}{2(d+K)^{2}}\right)+2\tau

Putting the above together with Equation (
33
) we get,

\displaystyle\min_{{\mathbf{C}}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{C}},{\boldsymbol{\phi}}({\mathbf{C}})\leq 0}\psi({\mathbf{C}})

\displaystyle\leq\min_{{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathcal{C}},{\boldsymbol{\phi}}({\mathbf{C}})\leq 0}\psi({\mathbf{C}})+\big(4a\sqrt{d+K}\big)\cdot\exp\left(\frac{-T}{2(d+K)^{2}}\right)+2\tau

where
\tau=a(\rho+2\sqrt{d}\rho^{\prime})
.
which completes the proof.

Lemma 46
.

Let
{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}^{*}\in\underset{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\in\Delta_{T},{\boldsymbol{\phi}}(\sum_{t}\alpha_{t}{\mathbf{C}}^{t})\leq 0}{\operatorname{argmin}}\psi\left(\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\alpha_{i}{\mathbf{C}}^{i}\right)
.
Then:

\displaystyle\psi\left(\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\alpha^{*}_{i}{\mathbf{C}}[h^{i}]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\min_{{\mathbf{C}}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{C}},{\boldsymbol{\phi}}({\mathbf{C}})\leq 0}\psi({\mathbf{C}})+2\tau;

\displaystyle\phi_{k}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\alpha^{*}_{i}{\mathbf{C}}[h^{i}]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\tau,

where
\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}=\textup{conv}(\{{\mathbf{C}}[h^{0}],\ldots,{\mathbf{C}}[h^{T-1}]\})
.

{proof}

Let
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\in\underset{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\Delta_{T},{\boldsymbol{\phi}}(\sum_{t}\beta_{t}{\mathbf{C}}[h^{t}])\leq 0}{\operatorname{argmin}}\psi\left(\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\beta_{i}{\mathbf{C}}[h^{i}]\right)

denote the coefficients obtained by solving a similar minimization problem with the estimates
{\mathbf{C}}^{t}
replaced with the true confusion matrices
C[h^{t}]
.
First, we note that
{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}^{*}
and
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}
exist because
h_{0}
(and in turn,
{\mathbf{C}}[h^{0}]={\mathbf{C}}^{0}
) is strictly feasible.

\displaystyle\psi\left(\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\alpha^{*}_{i}{\mathbf{C}}[h^{i}]\right)

\displaystyle=\psi\left(\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\alpha^{*}_{i}{\mathbf{C}}^{i}+\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\alpha^{*}_{i}({\mathbf{C}}[h^{i}]-{\mathbf{C}}^{i})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\psi\left(\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\alpha^{*}_{i}{\mathbf{C}}^{i}\right)+L\rho^{\prime}\sqrt{d}

\displaystyle=\min_{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\in\Delta_{T}}\psi\left(\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\alpha_{i}{\mathbf{C}}^{i}\right)+L\rho^{\prime}\sqrt{d}

\displaystyle\leq\psi\left(\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\beta^{*}_{i}{\mathbf{C}}^{i}\right)+L\rho^{\prime}\sqrt{d}

\displaystyle=\psi\left(\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\beta^{*}_{i}{\mathbf{C}}[h^{i}]+\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\beta^{*}_{i}({\mathbf{C}}^{i}-{\mathbf{C}}[h^{i}])\right)+L\rho^{\prime}\sqrt{d}

\displaystyle\leq\psi\left(\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\beta^{*}_{i}{\mathbf{C}}[h^{i}]\right)+2L\rho^{\prime}\sqrt{d}

\displaystyle=\min_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\Delta_{T},{\boldsymbol{\phi}}(\sum_{t}\beta_{t}{\mathbf{C}}[h^{t}])\leq 0}\psi\left(\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\beta_{i}{\mathbf{C}}[h^{i}]\right)+2L\rho^{\prime}\sqrt{d}

\displaystyle=\min_{{\mathbf{C}}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{C}},{\boldsymbol{\phi}}({\mathbf{C}})\leq 0}\psi({\mathbf{C}})+2L\rho^{\prime}\sqrt{d}

\displaystyle\leq\min_{{\mathbf{C}}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{C}},{\boldsymbol{\phi}}({\mathbf{C}})\leq 0}\psi({\mathbf{C}})+2\tau,

where the first and third inequality above are due to the Lipschitzness of
\psi
.

Using a similar argument as above, we get for all
k\in[K]
,

\displaystyle\phi_{k}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\alpha^{*}_{i}{\mathbf{C}}[h^{i}]\right)

\displaystyle=\phi_{k}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\alpha^{*}_{i}{\mathbf{C}}^{i}+\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\alpha^{*}_{i}({\mathbf{C}}[h^{i}]-{\mathbf{C}}^{i})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\phi_{k}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\alpha^{*}_{i}{\mathbf{C}}^{i}\right)+L\rho^{\prime}\sqrt{d}

\displaystyle\leq 0+L\rho^{\prime}\sqrt{d}\leq\tau,

where the first inequality above is due to the Lipschitzness of
\phi
, and the second inequality is due to the property of
{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}^{*}
being chosen from a set such that the weighted combination of
{\mathbf{C}}^{i}
is feasible.

We are now ready to prove Theorem
18
.

{proof}
[Proof of Theorem
18
]
Let
{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}^{*}\in\underset{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\in\Delta_{T},{\boldsymbol{\phi}}(\sum_{t}\alpha_{t}{\mathbf{C}}^{t})\leq{\mathbf{0}}}{\operatorname{argmin}}\psi\left(\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\alpha_{i}{\mathbf{C}}^{i}\right).
Let
\bar{d}=d+K
.
Putting Lemmas
45
and
46
together we get,

\displaystyle\psi({\mathbf{C}}[\overline{h}])

\displaystyle=\psi\left(\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\alpha^{*}_{i}{\mathbf{C}}[h^{i}]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\min_{{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathcal{C}},{\boldsymbol{\phi}}({\mathbf{C}})\leq 0}\psi({\mathbf{C}})+\big(4a\sqrt{\bar{d}}\big)\cdot\exp\left(\frac{-T}{2(\bar{d})^{2}}\right)+4\tau

\displaystyle=\min_{{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathcal{C}},{\boldsymbol{\phi}}({\mathbf{C}})\leq 0}\psi({\mathbf{C}})+\big(4a\sqrt{\bar{d}}\big)\cdot\exp\left(\frac{-T}{2(\bar{d})^{2}}\right)+4\tau

We now set
T=2\bar{d}^{2}\log\left(\frac{\bar{d}}{\epsilon}\right)
to obtain

\displaystyle\psi({\mathbf{C}}[\overline{h}])

\displaystyle\leq\min_{{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathcal{C}},{\boldsymbol{\phi}}({\mathbf{C}})\leq 0}\psi({\mathbf{C}})+\big(4a\big)\epsilon+4\tau

The feasibility inequality then follows easily from Lemma
46

\displaystyle\phi_{k}({\mathbf{C}}[\overline{h}])

\displaystyle=\phi_{k}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\alpha^{*}_{i}{\mathbf{C}}[h^{i}]\right)~~\leq\tau.

for all
k\in[K]
.

A.11
Proof of Theorem
19
(Bisection for Constrained Problems)

Theorem

((Restated) Convergence of ConBisection algorithm)
.

Fix
\epsilon\in(0,1)
.
Let
\psi:[0,1]^{d}{\rightarrow}[0,1]
be such that
\psi({\mathbf{C}})\,=\,\frac{\langle{\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{C}}\rangle}{\langle{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{C}}\rangle}
, where
{\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{B}}\in[0,1]^{d}
, and
\min_{{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathcal{C}}}{\langle{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{C}}\rangle}\,=\,b
for some
b>0
. Let
\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{K}:[0,1]^{d}{\rightarrow}[-1,1]
be convex and
L
-Lipschitz w.r.t. the
\ell_{2}
-norm. Let
\Omega
in Algorithm
8
be a
(\rho,\rho^{\prime},\delta)
-approximate LMO for sample size
N
.
Suppose the strict feasibility condition in Assumption
1
holds for radius
r>0
.
Let
\Lambda
,
\Xi
,
\eta
and
\eta^{\prime}
in the call to Algorithm
6
be set as in Theorem
17
with Lipschitz constant
L^{\prime}=\max\{L,\|{\mathbf{A}}\|_{2}+\|{\mathbf{B}}\|_{2}\}
.
Let
\bar{h}
be a classifier returned by Algorithm
8
when run for
T
outer iterations and
T^{\prime}
inner iterations. Then with probability
\geq 1-\delta
over draw of
S\sim D^{N}
, after
T=\log(1/\epsilon)
outer iterations and
T^{\prime}={\mathcal{O}}(K/\epsilon^{2})
inner iterations:

\textbf{Optimality}:~~\psi({\mathbf{C}}[\bar{h}])\,\leq\,\min_{{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathcal{C}}:\,{\boldsymbol{\phi}}({\mathbf{C}})\leq{\mathbf{0}}}\,\psi({\mathbf{C}})\,+\,{\mathcal{O}}\left(\kappa(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})\right);

\textbf{Feasibility}:~~\phi_{k}({\mathbf{C}}[\bar{h}])\,\leq\,{\mathcal{O}}\left(L^{\prime}(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})\right),~\forall k\in[K],

where
\kappa=L^{\prime}/b
and
\rho^{\textup{{eff}}}={\rho}+\sqrt{d}{\rho^{\prime}}
.

The proof follows similar steps as that for Theorem
15
.
We will first state a couple of lemmas:

Lemma 47

(Invariant in Algorithm
8
)
.

Under the assumptions made in Theorem
19
, the following invariant is true at the end of each iteration
0\leq t\leq T
of Algorithm
8
:

\alpha^{t}-{\mathcal{O}}\left(\kappa(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})\right)\,\leq\,\min_{{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathcal{C}}:\,\phi_{k}({\mathbf{C}})\leq 0,\forall k}\psi({\mathbf{C}})\,\leq\,\psi({\mathbf{C}}[h^{t}])\,<\,\beta^{t}+{\mathcal{O}}\left(\kappa(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})\right);

\phi_{k}({\mathbf{C}}[\bar{h}])\,\leq\,{\mathcal{O}}\left(L^{\prime}(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})\right),~\forall k\in[K].

where
L^{\prime}
,
\kappa
, and
\rho^{\textup{{eff}}}
are defined as in Theorem
19
.

{proof}

We shall prove this lemma by mathematical induction on the iteration number
t
. For
t=0
, the invariant holds trivially as
0\leq\psi({\mathbf{C}}[h^{0}])\leq 1
and
h^{0}
satisfies the constraints. Assume the invariant holds at the end of iteration
t-1\in\{0,\ldots,T-1\}
; we shall prove that the invariant holds at the end of iteration
t
.

First note that the
linear function
\psi^{\prime}({\mathbf{C}})=\langle{\mathbf{A}}-\gamma^{t}{\mathbf{B}},\,{\mathbf{C}}\rangle

in step 6 of the algorithm is Lipschitz w.r.t. the
\ell_{2}
-norm with Lipschitz parameter of at most
\|{\mathbf{A}}-\gamma^{t}{\mathbf{B}}\|_{2}\leq\|{\mathbf{A}}\|_{2}+\|{\mathbf{B}}\|_{2}\leq L^{\prime}
. We then have from Theorem
17
that the
classifier
g^{t}
returned by the ConGDA algorithm (Algorithm
6
) after
T^{\prime}={\mathcal{O}}(K/\epsilon^{2})
runs enjoys the following guarantee:

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{A}}-\gamma^{t}{\mathbf{B}},\,{\mathbf{C}}[{g}^{t}])\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\min_{{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathcal{C}}:\,\phi_{k}({\mathbf{C}})\leq 0,\forall k}\,\langle{\mathbf{A}}-\gamma^{t}{\mathbf{B}},\,{\mathbf{C}}\rangle\,+\,{\mathcal{O}}\left(L^{\prime}(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})\right);

(34)

\displaystyle\phi_{k}({\mathbf{C}}[g^{t}])

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}\left(L^{\prime}(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})\right),~\forall k\in[K],

(35)

where we have used the fact that both
\psi^{\prime}({\mathbf{C}})=\langle{\mathbf{A}}-\gamma^{t}{\mathbf{B}},\,{\mathbf{C}}\rangle
and
\phi_{k}({\mathbf{C}})
s are
L^{\prime}
-Lipschitz w.r.t. the
\ell_{2}
-norm.
We further have that from the property of the LMO (Definition
11
) used in turn by Algorithm
5
that:

\|{\mathbf{C}}^{t}-{\mathbf{C}}[g^{t}]\|_{\infty}\leq\rho^{\prime}

(36)

We now consider two cases at iteration
t
. In the first case,
\psi({\mathbf{C}}^{t})\leq\gamma^{t}
, leading to the assignments
\alpha^{t}=\alpha^{t-1}
,
\beta^{t}=\gamma^{t}
, and
h^{t}=g^{t}
. We have from (
36
) that:

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{A}}-\gamma^{t}{\mathbf{B}},\,{\mathbf{C}}[{g}^{t}])\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{A}}-\gamma^{t}{\mathbf{B}},\,{\mathbf{C}}^{t}\rangle\,+\,\|{\mathbf{A}}-\gamma^{t}{\mathbf{B}}\|_{1}\rho^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{B}},\,{\mathbf{C}}^{t}\rangle\big(\psi({\mathbf{C}}^{t})\,-\,\gamma^{t}\big)\,+\,\|{\mathbf{A}}-\gamma^{t}{\mathbf{B}}\|_{1}\rho^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 0\,+\,\|{\mathbf{A}}-\gamma^{t}{\mathbf{B}}\|_{1}\rho^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\|{\mathbf{A}}\|_{2}+\|{\mathbf{B}}\|_{2})\sqrt{d}\rho^{\prime}\,\leq\,L^{\prime}\sqrt{d}\rho^{\prime}\,<\,L^{\prime}(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}}),

where the third step follows from our case assumption that
\psi({\mathbf{C}}^{t})\leq\gamma^{t}
and
\langle{\mathbf{B}},\,{\mathbf{C}}^{t}\rangle>0
, and the last step follows from triangle inequality and
0\leq\gamma^{t}\leq 1
.
The above inequality further gives us

\displaystyle\frac{\langle{\mathbf{A}},\,{\mathbf{C}}[{g}^{t}]\rangle}{\langle{\mathbf{B}},\,{\mathbf{C}}[{g}^{t}]\rangle}\,<\,\gamma^{t}\,+\,{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{L^{\prime}}{b}(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})\right)\,<\,\beta^{t}\,+\,{\mathcal{O}}(\kappa(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})).

In other words,

\psi\big({\mathbf{C}}[h^{t}]\big)\,=\,\psi({\mathbf{C}}[{g}^{t}])\,=\,\frac{\langle{\mathbf{A}},\,{\mathbf{C}}^{D}[{g}^{t}]\rangle}{\langle{\mathbf{B}},\,{\mathbf{C}}^{D}[{g}^{t}]\rangle}\,<\,\beta^{t}\,+\,{\mathcal{O}}(\kappa(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})).

Moreover, by our assumption that the invariant holds at the end of iteration
t-1
, we have

\alpha^{t}\,-\,{\mathcal{O}}\left(\kappa(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})\right)\,=\,\alpha^{t-1}\,-\,{\mathcal{O}}\left(\kappa(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})\right)\,\leq\,\min_{{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathcal{C}}}\psi({\mathbf{C}})\,\leq\,\psi({\mathbf{C}}[h^{t}])\,<\,\beta^{t}\,+\,{\mathcal{O}}(\kappa(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})).

Further, from (
35
),
\phi_{k}({\mathbf{C}}[h^{t}])=\phi_{k}({\mathbf{C}}[g^{t}])\leq{\mathcal{O}}(L^{\prime}\bar{\rho}),\forall k
.
Thus under the first case, the invariant holds at the end of iteration
t
.

In the second case,
\psi({\mathbf{C}}^{t})>\gamma^{t}
at iteration
t
, which would lead to the assignments
\alpha^{t}=\gamma^{t}
,
\beta^{t}=\beta^{t-1}
, and
h^{t}=h^{t-1}
. Since the invariant is assumed to hold at the end of iteration
t-1
, we have

\beta^{t}+{\mathcal{O}}\left(\kappa(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})\right)\,=\,\beta^{t-1}+{\mathcal{O}}(\kappa(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}}))\,>\,\psi({\mathbf{C}}[h^{t-1}])\,=\,\psi({\mathbf{C}}[h^{t}]).

(37)

Next for
{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\,\in\,\underset{{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathcal{C}}:\,{\boldsymbol{\phi}}({\mathbf{C}})\leq{\mathbf{0}}}{\operatorname{argmin}}\,\,\langle{\mathbf{A}}-\gamma^{t}{\mathbf{B}},\,{\mathbf{C}}\rangle
, we have from (
34
),

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{A}}-\gamma^{t}{\mathbf{B}},\,{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{A}}-\gamma^{t}{\mathbf{B}},\,{\mathbf{C}}[h^{t}]\rangle\,-\,{\mathcal{O}}(L^{\prime}(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}}))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{A}}-\gamma^{t}{\mathbf{B}},\,{\mathbf{C}}^{t}\rangle\,-\,\|{\mathbf{A}}-\gamma^{t}{\mathbf{B}}\|_{1}\|{\mathbf{C}}^{t}-{\mathbf{C}}[h^{t}]\|_{\infty}\,-\,{\mathcal{O}}(L^{\prime}(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}}))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{A}}-\gamma^{t}{\mathbf{B}},\,{\mathbf{C}}^{t}\rangle\,-\,\|{\mathbf{A}}-\gamma^{t}{\mathbf{B}}\|_{1}\rho^{\prime}\,-\,{\mathcal{O}}(L^{\prime}(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{B}},\,{\mathbf{C}}^{t}\rangle(\psi({\mathbf{C}}^{t})-\gamma^{t})\,-\,\|{\mathbf{A}}-\gamma^{t}{\mathbf{B}}\|_{1}\rho^{\prime}\,-\,{\mathcal{O}}(L^{\prime}(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}}))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{B}},\,{\mathbf{C}}^{t}\rangle(0)\,-\,\|{\mathbf{A}}-\gamma^{t}{\mathbf{B}}\|_{1}\rho^{\prime}\,-\,{\mathcal{O}}(L^{\prime}(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}}))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-(\|{\mathbf{A}}\|_{2}+\|{\mathbf{B}}\|_{2})\sqrt{d}\rho^{\prime}\,-\,{\mathcal{O}}(L^{\prime}(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}}))\,=\,-{\mathcal{O}}(L^{\prime}(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})),

where the second step follows from the property of the LMO, the second step follows from Holder’s inequality, the third step using (
36
), the forth step follows from our case assumption that
\psi({\mathbf{C}}^{t})\geq\gamma^{t}
and
\langle{\mathbf{B}},\,{\mathbf{C}}^{t}\rangle>0
, the last step follows
from the fact that
\|\mathbf{z}\|_{1}\geq\|\mathbf{z}\|_{2}
and from the triangle inequality and that
0\leq\gamma^{t}\leq 1
.
In particular, we have for all
{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathcal{C}}
such that
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}({\mathbf{C}})\leq{\mathbf{0}}
,

\langle{\mathbf{A}}-\gamma^{t}{\mathbf{B}},\,{\mathbf{C}}\rangle\,\geq\,-{\mathcal{O}}(L^{\prime}(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})),

or

\frac{\langle{\mathbf{A}},\,{\mathbf{C}}\rangle}{\langle{\mathbf{B}},\,{\mathbf{C}}\rangle}\,\geq\,\gamma^{t}-{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{L^{\prime}}{\langle{\mathbf{B}},\,{\mathbf{C}}\rangle}(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})\right)\,\geq\,\gamma^{t}-{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{L^{\prime}}{b}(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})\right)\,=\,\gamma^{t}-{\mathcal{O}}\left(\kappa(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})\right).

or

\psi({\mathbf{C}})\,\geq\,\gamma^{t}\,-\,{\mathcal{O}}\left(\kappa(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})\right)\,=\,\alpha^{t}-{\mathcal{O}}\left(\kappa(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})\right).

By combining the above with (
37
), we can see that the invariant holds in iteration
t
under this case as well.
We also have
\phi_{k}({\mathbf{C}}[h^{t}])=\phi_{k}({\mathbf{C}}[h^{t-1}])\leq{\mathcal{O}}\left(L^{\prime}(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})\right),\forall k
.
This completes the proof of the lemma.

Lemma 48

(Multiplicative Progress in Each Iteration of Algorithm
8
)
.

Let
\psi
be as defined in Theorem
19
. Then the following is true in each iteration
1\leq t\leq T
of Algorithm
8
:

\beta^{t}-\alpha^{t}~=~\frac{1}{2}\big(\beta^{t-1}-\alpha^{t-1}\big).

{proof}

We consider two cases in each iteration of Algorithm
8
. If in an iteration
t\in\{1,\ldots,T\}
,

\psi({\mathbf{C}}^{t})\leq\gamma^{t}
, leading to the assignment
\beta^{t}=\gamma^{t}
, then

\displaystyle\beta^{t}-\alpha^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma^{t}-\alpha^{t-1}~=~\frac{\alpha^{t-1}+\beta^{t-1}}{2}-\alpha^{t-1}~=~\frac{1}{2}(\beta^{t-1}-\alpha^{t-1}).

On the other hand, if

\psi({\mathbf{C}}^{t})>\gamma^{t}
, leading to the assignment
\alpha^{t}=\gamma^{t}
, then

\displaystyle\beta^{t}-\alpha^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta^{t-1}-\gamma^{t}~=~\beta^{t-1}-\frac{\alpha^{t-1}+\beta^{t-1}}{2}~=~\frac{1}{2}\big(\beta^{t-1}-\alpha^{t-1}\big).

Thus in both cases, the statement of the lemma is seen to hold.

We are now ready to prove Theorem
19
.

{proof}
[Proof of Theorem
19
]
For the classifier
\bar{h}=h^{T}
output by Algorithm
8
after
T
iterations, we have from Lemma
47
,

\displaystyle\psi\big({\mathbf{C}}[h^{T}]\big)\,-\,\min_{{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathcal{C}}}\psi({\mathbf{C}})

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\beta^{t}-\alpha^{t}\,+\,{\mathcal{O}}(\kappa(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}}))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{-T}\big(\beta^{0}-\alpha^{0}\big)\,+\,{\mathcal{O}}(\kappa(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{-T}\big(1-0\big)\,+\,{\mathcal{O}}(\kappa(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{-T}\,+\,{\mathcal{O}}(\kappa(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})),

where the second step follows from Lemma
48
. We additionally have from Lemma
47
,

\phi_{k}({\mathbf{C}}[\bar{h}])\,\leq\,{\mathcal{O}}\left(L^{\prime}(\epsilon+\rho^{\textup{{eff}}})\right),~\forall k\in[K].
Setting
T=\log(1/\epsilon)
completes the proof.

A.12
Proof of Theorem
20

Theorem

((Restated) Regret bound for plug-in LMO)
.

Fix
\delta\in(0,1)
. Then with probability
\geq 1-\delta
over draw of sample
S\sim D^{N}
, for any loss matrix
{\mathbf{L}}\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{d}
with
\|{\mathbf{L}}\|_{\infty}=1
, the classifier and confusion matrix
(\widehat{g},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})
returned by Algorithm
9
satisfies:

\langle{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{C}}[\widehat{g}]\rangle\,\leq\,\min_{h:{\mathcal{X}}{\rightarrow}\Delta_{n}}\langle{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{C}}[h]\rangle+{\mathbf{E}}_{X}\big[\big\|\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}(X)\,-\,{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(X)\big\|_{1}\big];\vskip-5.0pt

\|{\mathbf{C}}[\widehat{g}]\,-\,\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\|_{\infty}\,\leq\,{\mathcal{O}}\bigg(\sqrt{\displaystyle\frac{d\log(n)\log(N)+\log(d/\delta)}{N}}\bigg).

{proof}

For simplicity, we will represent both
{\mathbf{L}}
and
{\mathbf{C}}
as
n\times n
matrices instead of flattened
n^{2}
-dimensional vectors.
Let us denote the columns of
{\mathbf{L}}
by
{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{1},\ldots,{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{n}
, where
\ell_{j}=[L_{1,j},L_{2,j},\ldots,L_{n,j}]^{\top}
.

We can then re-write:

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{C}}[{h}]\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}L_{ij}\,C_{ij}[h]~=\,\sum_{i,j}{\mathbf{E}}_{X}\left[\eta_{i}(X)\,L_{ij}\,{\mathbf{1}}(h(X)=j)\right]

\displaystyle=\,\sum_{j=1}^{n}{\mathbf{E}}_{X}\left[{\mathbf{1}}(h(X)=j)\,{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(X)^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{j}\right]~=\,{\mathbf{E}}_{X}\left[{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(X)^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{h(X)}\right].

Let
h^{*}
be the Bayes-optimal classifier for the linear metric
\langle{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{C}}[\widehat{h}]\rangle
.
For the first part, we bound the
{\mathbf{L}}
-regret as follows:

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{C}}[\widehat{g}]\rangle-\langle{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{C}}[h^{*}]\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbf{E}}_{X}\big[{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(X)^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{\widehat{g}(X)}\big]-{\mathbf{E}}_{X}\big[{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(X)^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{h^{*}(X)}\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbf{E}}_{X}\big[\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(X)^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{\widehat{g}(X)}\big]+{\mathbf{E}}_{X}\big[({\boldsymbol{\eta}}(X)-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(X)^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{\widehat{g}(X)}\big]-{\mathbf{E}}_{X}\big[{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(X)^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{h^{*}(X)}\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbf{E}}_{X}\big[\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(X)^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{h^{*}(X)}\big]+{\mathbf{E}}_{X}\big[({\boldsymbol{\eta}}(X)-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(X)^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{\widehat{g}(X)}\big]-{\mathbf{E}}_{X}\big[{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(X)^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{h^{*}(X)}\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbf{E}}_{X}\big[({\boldsymbol{\eta}}(X)-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(X))^{\top}({\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{\widehat{g}(X)}-{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{h^{*}(X)})\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbf{E}}_{X}\big[\big\|{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(X)-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(X)\big\|_{1}\cdot\big\|{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{\widehat{g}(X)}-{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{h^{*}(X)}\big\|_{\infty}\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbf{E}}_{X}\big[\big\|{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(X)-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(X)\big\|_{1}\big],

where in the
third step, we use the fact that

\widehat{g}(x)\,=\,\operatorname{argmin}^{*}_{j\in[n]}\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}(x)^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{j}
; in the
last step, we have use the fact that
\|{\mathbf{L}}\|_{\infty}=1
.

For the second part, we denote the class of all plug-in classifiers constructed from a fixed class-probability estimator
\widehat{\eta}
by:

{\mathcal{H}}=\left\{h:{\mathcal{X}}{\rightarrow}[n],\,h(x)\,=\,\operatorname{argmin}^{*}_{y\in[n]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{y}^{\top}\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}(x)\,|\,{\mathbf{L}}\in[0,1]^{n\times n}\right\},

and provide a uniform convergence bound over all classifiers in
{\mathcal{H}}
, and in turn applies to the classifier
\widehat{g}
output by Algorithm
9
.

For any
a,b\in[n]
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathbf{q}}}\left|\widehat{C}^{S}_{a,b}[h]-C_{a,b}[h]\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in{\mathcal{H}}}\left|\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left({\mathbf{1}}(y_{i}=a,h(x_{i})=b)-{\mathbf{E}}[{\mathbf{1}}(Y=a,h(X)=b)]\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in{\mathcal{H}}^{b}}\left|\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left({\mathbf{1}}(y_{i}=a,h(x_{i})=1)-{\mathbf{E}}[{\mathbf{1}}(Y=a,h(X)=1)]\right)\right|\;,

where for a fixed
b\in[n]
,

{\mathcal{H}}^{b}\,=\,\left\{h:{\mathcal{X}}{\rightarrow}\{0,1\}:\exists{\mathbf{L}}\in[0,1]^{n\times n},\forall x\in{\mathcal{X}},\,h(x)={\mathbf{1}}\left(b=\operatorname{argmin}^{*}_{y\in[n]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{y}^{\top}\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}(x)\right)\right\}.

The set
{\mathcal{H}}^{b}
can be seen as hypothesis class whose concepts are the intersection of
n
halfspaces in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
(corresponding to
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}(x)
) through the origin. Hence we have from Lemma 3.2.3 of
Blumer et al. 1989
that the VC-dimension of
{\mathcal{H}}^{b}
is at most
2n^{2}\log(3n)
.

From standard uniform convergence arguments we have that for each
a,b\in[n]
, the following holds with at least probability
1-\delta
(over draw of
S\sim D^{N}
),

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in{\mathcal{H}}}\left|\widehat{C}^{S}_{a,b}[h]-C_{a,b}[h]\right|\,\leq\,{\mathcal{O}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{n^{2}\log(n)\log(N)+\log(\frac{1}{\delta})}{N}}\right).

Applying union bound over all
a,b\in[n]
, we have that the following holds with probability
\geq 1-\delta
:

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{S}[\widehat{g}]-{\mathbf{C}}[\widehat{g}]\right|\right|_{\infty}\,\leq\,\sup_{h\in{\mathcal{H}}}\left|\left|\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{S}[h]-{\mathbf{C}}[h]\right|\right|_{\infty}\,\leq\,{\mathcal{O}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{n^{2}\log(n)\log(N)+\log(\frac{n^{2}}{\delta})}{N}}\right).

Plugging
d=n^{2}
completes the proof.

Appendix B
Additional Experimental Details

B.1
Hyper-parameter Selection

We run the Frank-Wolfe and GDA algorithms for 5000 LMO calls, and the ellipsoid algorithm for 1000 LMO calls. We run the constrained algorithms for 10000, 10000 and 1000 LMO calls respectively. The unconstrained Frank-Wolfe algorithm has no other hyper-parameters to tune.
The GDA algorithm has two step-size parameters
\eta
and
\eta^{\prime}
, which we tune using a two-dimensional grid-search over
\{0.001,0.01,0.1\}^{2}
, picking the parameters that yield the lowest objective on the training set. For the ellipsoid algorithm, we fix the initial ellipsoid radius
a
to 1000.

The constrained counterpart to the Frank-Wolfe algorithm (SplitFW) in Algorithm
5
has two additional hyper-parameters: the weight on the quadratic penalty
\zeta
, which we set to 10, and the step-size
\eta
, for which, we adopt the same schedule used by
Gidel et al. 2018
, and set it to 0.5 for first
T/3
iterations, 0.1 for the next
T/3
iterations, and 0.001 for the final
T/3
iterations. Additionally, we find it sufficient to avoid the explicit line search for
\gamma^{t}
in line 7 and instead set to
\frac{2}{t+2}
, akin to the standard Frank-Wolfe setup. For the constrained version of GDA algorithm, we set the step-sizes
\eta_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}=\eta_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}=\eta^{\prime}
, and tune
\eta_{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}
and
\eta^{\prime}
using the same the two-dimensional grid search used for unconstrained GDA, picking among those that satisfy the constraints on the training set, the ones with the least training objective (When none of the parameters satisfy the constraints, we pick the one with the minimum constraint violation). The hyper-parameters for the constrained ellipsoid algorithm were chosen in the same way as the unconstrained version. For TFCO, we tuned the learning rates for the model and constraint from
\{0.001,0.01,0.1\}
and ran it for
5000
iterations.

B.2
Additional Details for CIFAR Case Studies

Below, we list the five super-classes in the CIFAR-55 dataset described in Section
8.8
, and the 10 classes that each of them comprise of:
(i)
Flowers and Fruits:
Orchid, Poppy, Rose, Sunflower, Tulip, Mushroom, Orange, Pear, Apples, and Sweet Pepper.
(ii)
Aquatic Animals:
Beaver, Dolphin, Otter, Aquarium Fish, Ray, Flat Fish, Shark, Trout, Whale, and Seal.
(iii)
Household Items:
Clock, Bed, Chair, Couch, Keyboard, Telephone, Television, Wardrobe, Table, and Lamp.
(iv)
Large Outdoor Scenes:
Bridge, Castle, House, Road, Mountain, Skyscraper, Cloud, Forest, Plain, and Sea.
(v)

Mammals:
Camel, Cattle, Chimpanzee, Elephant, Kangaroo, Porcupine, Possum, Raccoon, Fox, and Skunk.
We employ standard data augmentation techniques on the CIFAR datasets by applying random crops and horizontal flips.

.
